Included in scored practice: Yes

A. AWS Fargate
B. Amazon EKS
C. Amazon ECS
D. AWS Lambda

Book answer: B
Reviewed answer: B
Review rationale: EKS provides a managed Kubernetes control plane. Node or Fargate capacity, cluster workload design and scaling still require configuration. ECS uses a different orchestrator, and Fargate is a compute option rather than the Kubernetes control plane itself.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Less suitable for this question. Fargate supplies serverless container capacity for supported ECS/EKS configurations. It is not a stand-alone Kubernetes control plane and application service scaling remains a configured policy. This item tests managed Kubernetes control plane rather than that alternative mechanism.
B: Correct. EKS provides a managed Kubernetes control plane. Node or Fargate capacity, cluster workload design and scaling still require configuration. ECS uses a different orchestrator, and Fargate is a compute option rather than the Kubernetes control plane itself.
C: Less suitable for this question. ECS schedules container services and tasks. The choice of Fargate or EC2 capacity affects host management; generic Docker packaging alone does not distinguish ECS from Kubernetes. This item tests managed Kubernetes control plane rather than that alternative mechanism.
D: Less suitable for this question. Lambda executes bounded event-driven functions with automatic concurrency scaling subject to limits. It is not persistent storage, general container orchestration or a universal substitute for long-running server workloads. This item tests managed Kubernetes control plane rather than that alternative mechanism.

AWS references:

C: Less suitable for this question. Transfer Acceleration changes the network path; stored-object encryption is configured independently. This item tests accelerated S3 transfers rather than that alternative mechanism.
D: Less suitable for this question. Acceleration does not compute analytics over the transferred data. This item tests accelerated S3 transfers rather than that alternative mechanism.

219. What should you use to optimize the cost of your Amazon EC2 instances for applications with predictable workloads?
Bank ID: H219 | Official task: 4.2 | Review: Reviewed
Included in scored practice: Yes

A. On-Demand Instances
B. Reserved Instances
C. Spot Instances
D. Dedicated Hosts

Book answer: B
Reviewed answer: B
Review rationale: Matching Reserved Instances can reduce eligible EC2 compute charges for predictable long-term usage. On-Demand avoids commitment, Spot tolerates interruptions, and Dedicated Hosts address host-control/licensing needs. A reservation is not the same as universally reserving capacity in every scope.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Less suitable for this question. On-Demand uses provisioned capacity without a long-term discount commitment. It fits changing demand when interruption risk is unacceptable, but does not guarantee unlimited immediately available capacity. This item tests Reserved Instance baseline discounts rather than that alternative mechanism.
B: Correct. Matching Reserved Instances can reduce eligible EC2 compute charges for predictable long-term usage. On-Demand avoids commitment, Spot tolerates interruptions, and Dedicated Hosts address host-control/licensing needs. A reservation is not the same as universally reserving capacity in every scope.
C: Less suitable for this question. Spot uses spare EC2 capacity at a discount with interruption and availability trade-offs. Variable demand alone is not proof that a workload tolerates interruption. This item tests Reserved Instance baseline discounts rather than that alternative mechanism.
D: Less suitable for this question. Dedicated Hosts provide physical host allocation for supported control/licensing needs. They are not automatically the cheapest burst compute or a guarantee of application HA. This item tests Reserved Instance baseline discounts rather than that alternative mechanism.

AWS references:
AWS documentation: Reserved Instance baseline discounts - https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/ec2-reserved-instances.html

220. Which AWS service allows you to create a high-performance, distributed file system for use with Amazon EC2?
Bank ID: H220 | Official task: 3.1 | Review: Review only
Included in scored practice: No

A. Amazon S3
B. Amazon EBS
C. Amazon EFS
D. Amazon FSx for Windows File Server

Book answer: C
Reviewed answer: No unique defensible answer as written
Review rationale: EFS is shared NFS storage and FSx for Windows supplies managed SMB files; either may be high performance for the relevant clients. The source omits protocol, operating system and throughput requirements, so it cannot declare one universally best distributed file system.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Review note. S3 is durable object storage. It is not a drop-in relational database, block volume, shared NFS file system or general execution engine. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
B: Review note. EBS is persistent AZ-scoped block storage and snapshots are recovery points. Snapshot scheduling/copying is a separate configured operation, not a continuously running database replica. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
C: Review note. EFS provides shared NFS storage for supported clients. Regional and One Zone durability/availability differ; it does not provide a native Windows SMB service. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
D: Review note. FSx for Windows File Server provides managed SMB file storage and Windows integration; its interface differs from EFS NFS and S3 object APIs. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.

AWS references:
AWS documentation: file-system requirements missing - https://docs.aws.amazon.com/efs/latest/ug/whatisefs.html

221. How can you ensure high availability of an Amazon RDS database instance across multiple AWS Regions?
Bank ID: H221 | Official task: 2.2 | Review: Review only
Included in scored practice: No

A. Use Multi-AZ deployments
B. Enable read replicas in a different Region
C. Use Amazon RDS with Amazon S3
D. Implement Amazon RDS with Amazon Route 53

Book answer: B
Reviewed answer: No unique defensible answer as written
Review rationale: A supported RDS cross-Region read replica supplies an asynchronous remote copy but does not promise automatic cross-Region failover for a conventional RDS DB instance. Multi-AZ remains within one Region. The source's ensure-HA language requires a promotion and traffic recovery design.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Review note. Conventional RDS Multi-AZ DB instances use a synchronous standby in another AZ for managed failover. The single standby is not readable and Multi-AZ does not mean cross-Region recovery. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
B: Review note. Read replicas offload eligible reads and typically replicate asynchronously. They do not increase primary write throughput and promotion/recovery is different from synchronous standby failover. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
C: Review note. S3 is durable object storage. It is not a drop-in relational database, block volume, shared NFS file system or general execution engine. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
D: Review note. Route 53 provides authoritative DNS and supported routing policies. Each policy has distinct selection rules; DNS routing does not provision application copies or replicate data. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.

AWS references:
AWS documentation: cross-Region replication versus failover - https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/USER_ReadRepl.html

D: Less suitable for this question. RDS manages supported relational database engines with optional HA and read-scaling configurations. Native backups, standby failover and read replicas are different mechanisms. This item tests unstructured object data rather than that alternative mechanism.

223. What is the purpose of Amazon Route 53's latency-based routing policy?
Bank ID: H223 | Official task: 3.4 | Review: Reviewed
Included in scored practice: Yes

A. Route traffic based on the geographic location of users
B. Distribute traffic evenly across multiple endpoints
C. Route traffic to the endpoint with the lowest latency
D. Perform DNS failover for high availability

Book answer: C
Reviewed answer: C
Review rationale: Route 53 latency routing uses AWS latency measurements to select a configured Region associated with a latency record. It does not mean simple geographical proximity or guarantee the lowest instantaneous latency to every arbitrary endpoint.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Less suitable for this question. Geolocation routing chooses based on user location categories; latency routing uses measured latency to configured Regions. This item tests latency-based DNS routing rather than that alternative mechanism.
B: Less suitable for this question. Equal/weighted traffic distribution is not the definition of latency routing. This item tests latency-based DNS routing rather than that alternative mechanism.
C: Correct. Route 53 latency routing uses AWS latency measurements to select a configured Region associated with a latency record. It does not mean simple geographical proximity or guarantee the lowest instantaneous latency to every arbitrary endpoint.
D: Less suitable for this question. Failover routing selects a recovery endpoint by primary/secondary health state rather than primarily by latency. This item tests latency-based DNS routing rather than that alternative mechanism.

AWS references:
AWS documentation: latency-based DNS routing - https://docs.aws.amazon.com/Route53/latest/DeveloperGuide/routing-policy.html

B. S3 Versioning
C. S3 Lifecycle Policies
D. S3 Cross-Region Replication

Book answer: B
Reviewed answer: B
Review rationale: S3 Versioning retains distinct versions of objects and uses delete markers for ordinary deletes. Authorized version deletion and lifecycle rules can still permanently remove versions. Object Lock restricts deletion of protected versions rather than being the versioning feature itself.

Reasons for each original option:

It is not the feature that creates every version or an access-frequency optimizer. This item tests object version preservation rather than that alternative mechanism.
B: Correct. S3 Versioning retains distinct versions of objects and uses delete markers for ordinary deletes. Authorized version deletion and lifecycle rules can still permanently remove versions. Object Lock restricts deletion of protected versions rather than being the versioning feature itself.
C: Less suitable for this question. S3 lifecycle rules manage transitions and expiration using configured rules such as object age. They do not by themselves monitor every object's access frequency. This item tests object version preservation rather than that alternative mechanism.
D: Less suitable for this question. S3 replication copies eligible objects under configured rules and permissions. Cross-Region copies improve geographic recovery, but replication is asynchronous and is not a historical backup by itself. This item tests object version preservation rather than that alternative mechanism.

Book answer: A

The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
D: Review note. CloudFront delivers and caches supported HTTP content at edge locations. It does not deploy regional compute, optimize SQL or provide arbitrary raw TCP acceleration. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.

D: Less suitable for this question. Primary/secondary failover is a separate routing policy. This item tests weighted DNS selection rather than that alternative mechanism.

258. What feature of Amazon S3 ensures the durability and availability of stored data?
Bank ID: H258 | Official task: 2.2 | Review: Review only
Included in scored practice: No

A. S3 Versioning
B. S3 Cross-Region Replication
C. S3 Lifecycle Policies
D. S3 Object Lock

Book answer: B
Reviewed answer: No unique defensible answer as written
Review rationale: S3 storage classes already define native durability and availability behavior; Cross-Region Replication adds a configured remote copy. It is not required to make every S3 object durable. The source wording confuses baseline storage redundancy with optional regional recovery.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Review note. Versioning preserves object versions to recover overwritten/deleted data. It does not encrypt bytes, lower transfer costs or create cross-Region copies by itself. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
B: Review note. S3 replication copies eligible objects under configured rules and permissions. Cross-Region copies improve geographic recovery, but replication is asynchronous and is not a historical backup by itself. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
C: Review note. S3 lifecycle rules manage transitions and expiration using configured rules such as object age. They do not by themselves monitor every object's access frequency. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
D: Review note. Object Lock prevents deletion/overwriting of retained protected object versions under the selected mode. It is not the feature that creates every version or an access-frequency optimizer. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.

C: Less suitable for this question. Encryption protects data and is separate from elastic compute capacity. This item tests Beanstalk scaling and load distribution rather than that alternative mechanism.

D. Amazon Aurora

Reviewed answer: A

D: Review note. Multiple Regions can support disaster recovery, but need data replication, workload deployment and traffic routing. Placement alone is not a complete HA design. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Correct. Kinesis Data Streams ingests and retains ordered records within shards for consumer applications. S3 stores objects and DynamoDB/RDS are databases; although databases can expose change streams, they are not the dedicated retained event-stream service offered.
B: Less suitable for this question. S3 is durable object storage. It is not a drop-in relational database, block volume, shared NFS file system or general execution engine. This item tests retained event streams rather than that alternative mechanism.
C: Less suitable for this question. DynamoDB is a managed key-value/document database with capacity modes and indexing choices. It is not a relational SQL engine or a general shared file service. This item tests retained event streams rather than that alternative mechanism.
D: Less suitable for this question. RDS manages supported relational database engines with optional HA and read-scaling configurations. Native backups, standby failover and read replicas are different mechanisms. This item tests retained event streams rather than that alternative mechanism.

AWS references:
AWS documentation: retained event streams - https://docs.aws.amazon.com/streams/latest/dev/introduction.html

D: Review note. Multi-AZ placement reduces an AZ failure dependency when sufficient redundant capacity and dependencies are present. It does not alone route requests or create regional copies. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.

C. Amazon EFS

B: Less suitable for this question. EBS is persistent AZ-scoped block storage and snapshots are recovery points. C: Less suitable for this question. EFS provides shared NFS storage for supported clients. Regional and One Zone durability/availability differ; it does not provide a native Windows SMB service. This item tests durable object storage rather than that alternative mechanism.
D: Less suitable for this question. FSx supplies purpose-built managed file systems. The selected engine/protocol and deployment type determine performance and availability. This item tests durable object storage rather than that alternative mechanism.

D: Less suitable for this question. Savings Plans discount eligible committed hourly usage. Scope and flexibility differ from Reserved Instances; unused commitments still cost money and a purchase plan does not scale instance count. 308. What is the most cost-effective storage solution for long-term archival data that is infrequently accessed?

A. Amazon S3 Standard
B. Amazon S3 Intelligent-Tiering
C. Amazon S3 Glacier
D. Amazon EBS Standard

Reviewed answer: No unique defensible answer as written
Review rationale: The listed generic S3 Glacier name denotes archival storage but does not distinguish Instant Retrieval, Flexible Retrieval or Deep Archive. Minimum retention, retrieval requirements and data size affect total cost. It is an archival choice but not enough information for an absolute most-cost-effective claim.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Review note. S3 Standard targets frequent object access with regional multi-AZ redundancy. It is not a shared POSIX file interface or a block volume. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
B: Review note. Intelligent-Tiering moves eligible S3 objects between access tiers based on observed access; optional archival tiers require configuration and have restore trade-offs. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
C: Review note. The Glacier storage classes target archival patterns with class-specific retrieval behavior. The generic name does not specify Instant, Flexible or Deep Archive performance/costs. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
D: Review note. EBS is persistent AZ-scoped block storage and snapshots are recovery points. Snapshot scheduling/copying is a separate configured operation, not a continuously running database replica. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.

314. Which AWS service can help you to lower your data transfer costs between your VPC and the internet?
Bank ID: H314 | Official task: 4.4 | Review: Review only
Included in scored practice: No

A. Amazon CloudFront
B. AWS Global Accelerator
C. Amazon S3 Transfer Acceleration
D. AWS Direct Connect

Book answer: D
Reviewed answer: No unique defensible answer as written
Review rationale: Direct Connect serves a dedicated hybrid circuit, not a generic replacement for VPC internet traffic. CloudFront may reduce origin traffic for cacheable content; accelerators can add fees. The required destination and volume are missing, so no listed service guarantees lower internet transfer costs.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Review note. CloudFront delivers and caches supported HTTP content at edge locations. It does not deploy regional compute, optimize SQL or provide arbitrary raw TCP acceleration. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
B: Review note. Global Accelerator optimizes supported global network paths to configured healthy endpoints. It is not an application deployment service, a content cache or a guarantee of the geographically nearest Region. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
C: Review note. S3 Transfer Acceleration can speed suitable long-distance S3 transfers through edge-network paths; it is not a viewer content cache or a universal latency guarantee. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
D: Review note. Direct Connect supplies dedicated private hybrid connectivity, not encryption by default. A private circuit is not a general internet CDN or migration tool by itself. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.

AWS references:
AWS documentation: internet delivery versus dedicated hybrid transport - https://docs.aws.amazon.com/directconnect/latest/UserGuide/Welcome.html

B: Less suitable for this question. Cost Explorer provides interactive cost/usage analysis, forecasts and supported purchase/rightsizing recommendations. It can overlap with Advisor for optimization but is not a universal auto-remediator. This item tests configuration inventory rather than that alternative mechanism.
C: Less suitable for this question. Systems Manager supports fleet operations and configured automation. It can run custom workflows but is not a built-in universal snapshot optimizer or every security enforcement mechanism. This item tests configuration inventory rather than that alternative mechanism.
D: Less suitable for this question. Trusted Advisor provides supported best-practice and cost checks. It recommends actions rather than automatically eliminating every unused resource. This item tests configuration inventory rather than that alternative mechanism.

326. Which AWS feature helps optimize costs by automatically scaling your resources up or down based on demand?
Bank ID: H326 | Official task: 4.2 | Review: Reviewed
Included in scored practice: Yes

A. Auto Scaling
B. EC2 Spot Instances
C. EC2 Savings Plans
D. Reserved Instances

Book answer: A
Reviewed answer: A
Review rationale: Configured Auto Scaling changes instance count to match capacity demand and can reduce idle instance-hours. Discounts change unit prices rather than the number of resources. Policies, minimum bounds and workload metrics must be configured.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Correct. Configured Auto Scaling changes instance count to match capacity demand and can reduce idle instance-hours. Discounts change unit prices rather than the number of resources. Policies, minimum bounds and workload metrics must be configured.
B: Less suitable for this question. Spot uses spare EC2 capacity at a discount with interruption and availability trade-offs. Variable demand alone is not proof that a workload tolerates interruption. This item tests right-sized instance count rather than that alternative mechanism.
C: Less suitable for this question. Savings Plans discount eligible committed hourly usage. Scope and flexibility differ from Reserved Instances; unused commitments still cost money and a purchase plan does not scale instance count. This item tests right-sized instance count rather than that alternative mechanism.
D: Less suitable for this question. Reserved Instances discount eligible matching instance usage over a commitment term. They do not automatically scale capacity or outperform every Savings Plan/Spot option for every workload. This item tests right-sized instance count rather than that alternative mechanism.

328. What is the most cost-effective way to store data that is archived but needs to be restored occasionally?
Bank ID: H328 | Official task: 4.1 | Review: Review only
Included in scored practice: No

A. Amazon S3 Standard
B. Amazon S3 One Zone-IA
C. Amazon S3 Glacier
D. Amazon EBS

Book answer: C
Reviewed answer: No unique defensible answer as written
Review rationale: Glacier Flexible Retrieval and Deep Archive have different restore times, minimum storage durations and request/retrieval charges; Standard-IA can be appropriate when immediate reads are needed. Occasional access alone cannot establish the most cost-effective class without retention, size and restore requirements.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Review note. S3 Standard targets frequent object access with regional multi-AZ redundancy. It is not a shared POSIX file interface or a block volume. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
B: Review note. S3 One Zone-IA has millisecond retrieval but stores data in one AZ. Use it only when that resilience trade-off and retrieval/minimum-duration charges fit. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
C: Review note. The Glacier storage classes target archival patterns with class-specific retrieval behavior. The generic name does not specify Instant, Flexible or Deep Archive performance/costs. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
D: Review note. EBS is persistent AZ-scoped block storage and snapshots are recovery points. Snapshot scheduling/copying is a separate configured operation, not a continuously running database replica. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.

AWS references:
AWS documentation: archive retrieval requirements omitted - https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/storage-class-intro.html

D: Review note. Transit Gateway interconnects routed networks through a hub. It requires routing/segmentation design and can add attachment and processing costs. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.

343. Which AWS service can help you reduce data transfer costs by caching content closer to your users?
Bank ID: H343 | Official task: 4.4 | Review: Reviewed
Included in scored practice: Yes

A. Amazon CloudFront
B. AWS Global Accelerator
C. AWS Direct Connect
D. Amazon VPC PrivateLink

Book answer: A
Reviewed answer: A
Review rationale: CloudFront caches eligible HTTP content near viewers and can reduce origin fetches while improving latency. Global Accelerator optimizes network paths without caching, Direct Connect is hybrid connectivity, and S3 Transfer Acceleration targets transfer paths. Total CDN cost still depends on traffic and cacheability.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Correct. CloudFront caches eligible HTTP content near viewers and can reduce origin fetches while improving latency. Global Accelerator optimizes network paths without caching, Direct Connect is hybrid connectivity, and S3 Transfer Acceleration targets transfer paths. Total CDN cost still depends on traffic and cacheability.
B: Less suitable for this question. Global Accelerator optimizes supported global network paths to configured healthy endpoints. It is not an application deployment service, a content cache or a guarantee of the geographically nearest Region. This item tests CDN delivery and origin offload rather than that alternative mechanism.
C: Less suitable for this question. Direct Connect supplies dedicated private hybrid connectivity, not encryption by default. A private circuit is not a general internet CDN or migration tool by itself. This item tests CDN delivery and origin offload rather than that alternative mechanism.
D: Less suitable for this question. VPC endpoints provide private paths to supported services. IAM and TLS requirements still apply; interface endpoints and gateway endpoints have different costs and capabilities. This item tests CDN delivery and origin offload rather than that alternative mechanism.

AWS references:
AWS documentation: CDN delivery and origin offload - https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonCloudFront/latest/DeveloperGuide/Introduction.html

344. Which AWS feature allows you to save costs by using capacity that is otherwise unused in the AWS cloud?
Bank ID: H344 | Official task: 4.2 | Review: Reviewed
Included in scored practice: Yes

A. EC2 Spot Instances
B. EC2 Reserved Instances
C. AWS Savings Plans
D. EC2 On-Demand Instances

Book answer: A
Reviewed answer: A
Review rationale: EC2 Spot uses spare capacity at discounted rates and can be interrupted. It suits workloads designed for retry or checkpointing; it does not guarantee continuous availability, more CPU per instance, or dedicated hardware.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Correct. EC2 Spot uses spare capacity at discounted rates and can be interrupted. It suits workloads designed for retry or checkpointing; it does not guarantee continuous availability, more CPU per instance, or dedicated hardware.
B: Less suitable for this question. Reserved Instances discount eligible matching instance usage over a commitment term. They do not automatically scale capacity or outperform every Savings Plan/Spot option for every workload. This item tests interruptible spare capacity rather than that alternative mechanism.
C: Less suitable for this question. Savings Plans discount eligible committed hourly usage. Scope and flexibility differ from Reserved Instances; unused commitments still cost money and a purchase plan does not scale instance count. This item tests interruptible spare capacity rather than that alternative mechanism.
D: Less suitable for this question. On-Demand uses provisioned capacity without a long-term discount commitment. It fits changing demand when interruption risk is unacceptable, but does not guarantee unlimited immediately available capacity. This item tests interruptible spare capacity rather than that alternative mechanism.

346. Which AWS storage service provides an affordable option for backup and restore with a low retrieval time?
Bank ID: H346 | Official task: 4.1 | Review: Review only
Included in scored practice: No

A. Amazon S3 Glacier
B. Amazon S3 Intelligent-Tiering
C. Amazon EBS
D. Amazon S3 Standard

Book answer: A
Reviewed answer: No unique defensible answer as written
Review rationale: Generic S3 Glacier could mean several classes with very different retrieval times; S3 Standard and active Intelligent-Tiering tiers give immediate reads. Low retrieval time and affordable backup require explicit latency, retention and access-volume constraints, so the source key cannot be uniquely justified.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Review note. The Glacier storage classes target archival patterns with class-specific retrieval behavior. The generic name does not specify Instant, Flexible or Deep Archive performance/costs. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
B: Review note. Intelligent-Tiering moves eligible S3 objects between access tiers based on observed access; optional archival tiers require configuration and have restore trade-offs. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
C: Review note. EBS is persistent AZ-scoped block storage and snapshots are recovery points. Snapshot scheduling/copying is a separate configured operation, not a continuously running database replica. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
D: Review note. S3 Standard targets frequent object access with regional multi-AZ redundancy. It is not a shared POSIX file interface or a block volume. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.

AWS references:
AWS documentation: restore latency is unspecified - https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/storage-class-intro.html

C: Review note. Intelligent-Tiering moves eligible S3 objects between access tiers based on observed access; optional archival tiers require configuration and have restore trade-offs. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
D: Review note. EBS is persistent AZ-scoped block storage and snapshots are recovery points. Snapshot scheduling/copying is a separate configured operation, not a continuously running database replica. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.

361. Which AWS feature helps you minimize costs by ensuring that resources are only running when needed?
Bank ID: H361 | Official task: 4.2 | Review: Corrected
Included in scored practice: Yes

A. Auto Scaling
B. EC2 Spot Instances
C. EC2 Reserved Instances
D. AWS Savings Plans

Book answer: A
Reviewed answer: A
Review rationale: Configured scaling or scheduled actions can reduce unneeded EC2 capacity when demand falls. An Auto Scaling group must have suitable minimum capacity and policies; it does not automatically detect and stop every idle instance in the account. The practice stem makes the group/configuration condition explicit. Practice stem clarified; original preserved below.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Correct. Configured scaling or scheduled actions can reduce unneeded EC2 capacity when demand falls. An Auto Scaling group must have suitable minimum capacity and policies; it does not automatically detect and stop every idle instance in the account. The practice stem makes the group/configuration condition explicit.
B: Less suitable for this question. Spot uses spare EC2 capacity at a discount with interruption and availability trade-offs. Variable demand alone is not proof that a workload tolerates interruption. This item tests scaling down unused capacity rather than that alternative mechanism.
C: Less suitable for this question. Reserved Instances discount eligible matching instance usage over a commitment term. They do not automatically scale capacity or outperform every Savings Plan/Spot option for every workload. This item tests scaling down unused capacity rather than that alternative mechanism.
D: Less suitable for this question. Savings Plans discount eligible committed hourly usage. Scope and flexibility differ from Reserved Instances; unused commitments still cost money and a purchase plan does not scale instance count. This item tests scaling down unused capacity rather than that alternative mechanism.

B. AWS Direct Connect

C: Review note. DataSync automates supported online storage transfers, with network, compatibility and scheduling requirements. It is not an offline shipped device or a relational change-capture engine. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
D: Review note. Transfer Family supports managed file-transfer protocol endpoints. It is not universally cheaper than bulk online synchronization or physical offline transfer. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.

364. What is the cost-effective solution for storing data that is infrequently accessed but needs to be retained for long periods?
Bank ID: H364 | Official task: 4.1 | Review: Review only
Included in scored practice: No

A. Amazon S3 Glacier Deep Archive
B. Amazon S3 Glacier
C. Amazon S3 Standard-IA
D. Amazon EBS

Book answer: A
Reviewed answer: No unique defensible answer as written
Review rationale: Glacier Flexible Retrieval and Deep Archive have different restore times, minimum storage durations and request/retrieval charges; Standard-IA can be appropriate when immediate reads are needed. Occasional access alone cannot establish the most cost-effective class without retention, size and restore requirements.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Review note. S3 Glacier Deep Archive targets very cold long-duration retention and needs asynchronous restoration. Minimum duration and retrieval charges matter; it is not for immediate reads. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
B: Review note. The Glacier storage classes target archival patterns with class-specific retrieval behavior. The generic name does not specify Instant, Flexible or Deep Archive performance/costs. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
C: Review note. S3 Standard-IA combines infrequent-access pricing with multi-AZ storage and millisecond retrieval, with retrieval and minimum-duration charges. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
D: Review note. EBS is persistent AZ-scoped block storage and snapshots are recovery points. Snapshot scheduling/copying is a separate configured operation, not a continuously running database replica. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.

AWS documentation: archive retrieval requirements omitted - https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/storage-class-intro.html

Global Accelerator optimizes network paths without caching, Direct Connect is hybrid connectivity, and S3 Transfer Acceleration targets transfer paths. Reasons for each original option:

Global Accelerator optimizes network paths without caching, Direct Connect is hybrid connectivity, and S3 Transfer Acceleration targets transfer paths. Total CDN cost still depends on traffic and cacheability.
B: Less suitable for this question. Global Accelerator optimizes supported global network paths to configured healthy endpoints. It is not an application deployment service, a content cache or a guarantee of the geographically nearest Region. This item tests CDN delivery and origin offload rather than that alternative mechanism.
C: Less suitable for this question. Direct Connect supplies dedicated private hybrid connectivity, not encryption by default. A private circuit is not a general internet CDN or migration tool by itself. This item tests CDN delivery and origin offload rather than that alternative mechanism.
D: Less suitable for this question. S3 Transfer Acceleration can speed suitable long-distance S3 transfers through edge-network paths; it is not a viewer content cache or a universal latency guarantee. This item tests CDN delivery and origin offload rather than that alternative mechanism.

B: Less suitable for this question. Spot uses spare EC2 capacity at a discount with interruption and availability trade-offs. Variable demand alone is not proof that a workload tolerates interruption. This item tests right-sized instance count rather than that alternative mechanism.
C: Less suitable for this question. Reserved Instances discount eligible matching instance usage over a commitment term. They do not automatically scale capacity or outperform every Savings Plan/Spot option for every workload. This item tests right-sized instance count rather than that alternative mechanism.
D: Less suitable for this question. Savings Plans discount eligible committed hourly usage. Scope and flexibility differ from Reserved Instances; unused commitments still cost money and a purchase plan does not scale instance count. This item tests right-sized instance count rather than that alternative mechanism.

AWS references:
AWS documentation: right-sized instance count - https://docs.aws.amazon.com/autoscaling/ec2/userguide/what-is-amazon-ec2-auto-scaling.html

381. What is the most cost-effective AWS service for delivering content to end users with low latency?
Bank ID: H381 | Official task: 4.4 | Review: Reviewed
Included in scored practice: Yes

A. Amazon CloudFront
B. AWS Global Accelerator
C. AWS Direct Connect
D. Amazon S3 Transfer Acceleration

Book answer: A
Reviewed answer: A
Review rationale: CloudFront caches eligible HTTP content near viewers and can reduce origin fetches while improving latency. Global Accelerator optimizes network paths without caching, Direct Connect is hybrid connectivity, and S3 Transfer Acceleration targets transfer paths. Total CDN cost still depends on traffic and cacheability.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Correct. CloudFront caches eligible HTTP content near viewers and can reduce origin fetches while improving latency. Global Accelerator optimizes network paths without caching, Direct Connect is hybrid connectivity, and S3 Transfer Acceleration targets transfer paths. Total CDN cost still depends on traffic and cacheability.
B: Less suitable for this question. Global Accelerator optimizes supported global network paths to configured healthy endpoints. It is not an application deployment service, a content cache or a guarantee of the geographically nearest Region. This item tests CDN delivery and origin offload rather than that alternative mechanism.
C: Less suitable for this question. Direct Connect supplies dedicated private hybrid connectivity, not encryption by default. A private circuit is not a general internet CDN or migration tool by itself. This item tests CDN delivery and origin offload rather than that alternative mechanism.
D: Less suitable for this question. S3 Transfer Acceleration can speed suitable long-distance S3 transfers through edge-network paths; it is not a viewer content cache or a universal latency guarantee. This item tests CDN delivery and origin offload rather than that alternative mechanism.

AWS references:
AWS documentation: CDN delivery and origin offload - https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonCloudFront/latest/DeveloperGuide/Introduction.html

385. Which AWS storage class is the most cost-effective for storing data that is accessed less frequently and requires rapid retrieval?
Bank ID: H385 | Official task: 4.1 | Review: Review only
Included in scored practice: No

A. Amazon S3 One Zone-IA
B. Amazon S3 Glacier
C. Amazon S3 Intelligent-Tiering
D. Amazon S3 Standard

Book answer: A
Reviewed answer: No unique defensible answer as written
Review rationale: One Zone-IA supplies millisecond object access at reduced storage cost for an appropriate infrequent-access workload, but stores data in one AZ and charges retrieval/minimum durations. Without allowing loss of that AZ copy or specifying recreatable data, it is not universally the correct low-cost choice.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Review note. S3 One Zone-IA has millisecond retrieval but stores data in one AZ. Use it only when that resilience trade-off and retrieval/minimum-duration charges fit. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
B: Review note. The Glacier storage classes target archival patterns with class-specific retrieval behavior. The generic name does not specify Instant, Flexible or Deep Archive performance/costs. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
C: Review note. Intelligent-Tiering moves eligible S3 objects between access tiers based on observed access; optional archival tiers require configuration and have restore trade-offs. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
D: Review note. S3 Standard targets frequent object access with regional multi-AZ redundancy. It is not a shared POSIX file interface or a block volume. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.

AWS references:

390. Which AWS storage service is best for data that is archived but needs to be accessed occasionally?
Bank ID: H390 | Official task: 4.1 | Review: Review only
Included in scored practice: No

A. Amazon S3 Glacier
B. Amazon S3 Intelligent-Tiering
C. Amazon S3 One Zone-IA
D. Amazon EBS

Reviewed answer: No unique defensible answer as written
Review rationale: Glacier Flexible Retrieval and Deep Archive have different restore times, minimum storage durations and request/retrieval charges; Standard-IA can be appropriate when immediate reads are needed. Occasional access alone cannot establish the most cost-effective class without retention, size and restore requirements.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Review note. The Glacier storage classes target archival patterns with class-specific retrieval behavior. The generic name does not specify Instant, Flexible or Deep Archive performance/costs. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
B: Review note. Intelligent-Tiering moves eligible S3 objects between access tiers based on observed access; optional archival tiers require configuration and have restore trade-offs. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.

D: Review note. EBS is persistent AZ-scoped block storage and snapshots are recovery points. Snapshot scheduling/copying is a separate configured operation, not a continuously running database replica. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.

CUR provides detailed exported records, Budgets monitors thresholds, and Trusted Advisor provides best-practice checks. Cost Explorer does not automatically make every resource cheaper.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Correct. Cost Explorer provides interactive cost and usage views, grouping, forecasts and supported purchase recommendations. CUR provides detailed exported records, Budgets monitors thresholds, and Trusted Advisor provides best-practice checks. Cost Explorer does not automatically make every resource cheaper.
B: Less suitable for this question. CUR supplies detailed billing/usage exports for downstream analysis. Interactive views and threshold actions are separate cost-management capabilities. This item tests interactive cost analysis rather than that alternative mechanism.
C: Less suitable for this question. Budgets monitors configured spending/usage thresholds and can send alerts or configured supported actions. It is not a universal instantaneous hard spending cap. This item tests interactive cost analysis rather than that alternative mechanism.
D: Less suitable for this question. Trusted Advisor provides supported best-practice and cost checks. It recommends actions rather than automatically eliminating every unused resource. This item tests interactive cost analysis rather than that alternative mechanism.

AWS references:
AWS documentation: interactive cost analysis - https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/ce-what-is.html

397. What is the most cost-effective AWS service for delivering static and dynamic content to users globally with minimal latency?
Bank ID: H397 | Official task: 4.4 | Review: Reviewed
Included in scored practice: Yes

A. Amazon CloudFront
B. AWS Global Accelerator
C. AWS Direct Connect
D. AWS S3 Transfer Acceleration

Book answer: A
Reviewed answer: A
Review rationale: CloudFront caches eligible HTTP content near viewers and can reduce origin fetches while improving latency. Global Accelerator optimizes network paths without caching, Direct Connect is hybrid connectivity, and S3 Transfer Acceleration targets transfer paths. Total CDN cost still depends on traffic and cacheability.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Correct. CloudFront caches eligible HTTP content near viewers and can reduce origin fetches while improving latency. Global Accelerator optimizes network paths without caching, Direct Connect is hybrid connectivity, and S3 Transfer Acceleration targets transfer paths. Total CDN cost still depends on traffic and cacheability.
B: Less suitable for this question. Global Accelerator optimizes supported global network paths to configured healthy endpoints. It is not an application deployment service, a content cache or a guarantee of the geographically nearest Region. This item tests CDN delivery and origin offload rather than that alternative mechanism.
C: Less suitable for this question. Direct Connect supplies dedicated private hybrid connectivity, not encryption by default. A private circuit is not a general internet CDN or migration tool by itself. This item tests CDN delivery and origin offload rather than that alternative mechanism.
D: Less suitable for this question. S3 Transfer Acceleration can speed suitable long-distance S3 transfers through edge-network paths; it is not a viewer content cache or a universal latency guarantee. This item tests CDN delivery and origin offload rather than that alternative mechanism.

AWS references:
AWS documentation: CDN delivery and origin offload - https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonCloudFront/latest/DeveloperGuide/Introduction.html

400. What is the most cost-effective AWS service for storing and retrieving large amounts of infrequently accessed data?
Bank ID: H400 | Official task: 4.1 | Review: Review only
Included in scored practice: No

A. Amazon S3 Glacier
B. Amazon S3 Glacier Deep Archive
C. Amazon S3 Standard-IA
D. Amazon EBS

Book answer: A
Reviewed answer: No unique defensible answer as written
Review rationale: Glacier Flexible Retrieval and Deep Archive have different restore times, minimum storage durations and request/retrieval charges; Standard-IA can be appropriate when immediate reads are needed. Occasional access alone cannot establish the most cost-effective class without retention, size and restore requirements.

Reasons for each original option:
A: Review note. The Glacier storage classes target archival patterns with class-specific retrieval behavior. The generic name does not specify Instant, Flexible or Deep Archive performance/costs. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
B: Review note. S3 Glacier Deep Archive targets very cold long-duration retention and needs asynchronous restoration. Minimum duration and retrieval charges matter; it is not for immediate reads. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
C: Review note. S3 Standard-IA combines infrequent-access pricing with multi-AZ storage and millisecond retrieval, with retrieval and minimum-duration charges. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.
D: Review note. EBS is persistent AZ-scoped block storage and snapshots are recovery points. Snapshot scheduling/copying is a separate configured operation, not a continuously running database replica. The source wording does not make this option uniquely wrong; see the item review above.

AWS references:
AWS documentation: archive retrieval requirements omitted - https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/storage-class-intro.html
